STIR-L4 · a worked example

The complete pipeline

One simulated plan, from the first question to the final report: five sessions, every reply as written

Every STIR-L4 planning effort ends with a final report: the plan as the team certified it, followed by a protocol written in the standard template for systematic review protocols (PRISMA-P 2015). Part 1 is that report for this run, as the assistant produced it, tidied for readers (see Fidelity below). Part 2 is the conversation that led to it, so that readers can see how each decision was reached. The user, a simulated solo reviewer, plans a review of what kind of weight is lost with SGLT2 inhibitors.

About this run

  • User: a second AI playing Uday, a terse solo reviewer, from a script the first author approved (eval/personas/P1_full_pipeline.md).
  • Assistant: Claude Opus 5.5 (Anthropic), given only the STIR-L4 prompt and the files a team would upload: the prompt, the helper kit, the model file and the core-properties file. The sessions were run while the prompt was being finalized, on development versions that differ from the released prompt (version 1.0, Supplementary File 1) in how the assistant talks with the team and writes the report, not in the STIR-L4 model.
  • Searches: real. 169 logged retrievals from PubMed (including its MeSH index and PubMed Central), OpenAlex and ClinicalTrials.gov, run live on 25 October 2026, each saved; every reference in the protocol was found in a saved search result and checked again against PubMed and Crossref.
  • What the report leaves out: STIR-L4 in a chat app plans a review; it does not screen studies or extract data. The pilot screening and the commitment in Sessions 3 and 4 followed the prompt's steps at the time and tested the criteria; they stay in the log. Paper as an Object, Literature as a Tensor and Registering Evidence Dynamically were designed at Step 7 but are not built in the prompt version; they are being developed for a dedicated planning tool.
  • Fidelity of this document: every message is reproduced word for word, so drafts in the conversation carry working version numbers. The report in Part 1 is the file the helper kit wrote, with editorial changes only, each listed in docs/example/clean_report.py: working version labels and draft history removed, every PMID and DOI linked, and the plan's Methodology entry showing the methods as certified rather than the protocol's certification line. No content was changed. Only the layout and the lettered notes are added.

Contents

  • Part 1, the STIR-L4 report: 1. the plan, as certified; 2. the protocol (PRISMA-P items, what planning found, how STIR-L4 and AI were used, gaps, references).
  • Part 2, the conversation: Session 1 (steps 1 to 3), Session 2 (3 to 6), Session 3 (6 to 9), Session 4 (10 to 12), Session 5 (the protocol and the final report).

Margin notes

  1. RResuming a session from saved files
  2. SThe soundness check of the question
  3. FFeedback between decisions (reopening)
  4. CCertifications and progress
  5. LBefore the search: extraction form, registry, atlas
  6. VVerification and safeguards
  7. PPilot screening by the person (a test of the criteria)
  8. HHonesty about what was not done
  9. WWriting the protocol and the final report

Session 1 keeps the numbered notes of the first example (1 to 9).

Part 1. The STIR-L4 report

From the assistant's planning folder (reports/2026-10-25_final_report.md, written by the helper kit from the saved plan and the certified protocol), with the editorial changes listed on the cover. Every PMID and DOI is a link.

STIR-L4 planning report: which kind of weight is lost: fat, muscle or body water, within all adults without diabetes, whatever the drug is given for, any country; Asian people as a subgroup

Planning team: Uday. Planning record: 783 entries, 2026-10-25 to 2026-10-25. STIR-L4 prompt and helper kit: development versions, before release 1.0; AI model: Anthropic Claude Opus 5.5 claude-opus-5-5 (as recorded: interface, read by Uday).

1. The plan, as certified

Topic: the role of SGLT2 inhibitors in the weight lost by all adults without diabetes, whatever the drug is given for, any country; Asian people as a subgroup

Research focus: which kind of weight is lost: fat, muscle or body water, within all adults without diabetes, whatever the drug is given for, any country; Asian people as a subgroup

Individual Motivation

Certified by Uday on 2026-10-25 at 03:21 UTC (L000058); reason, as given: "testing the workflow"

Uday (summarized): interest — which kind of weight is lost: fat or muscle; seeks — to learn; kinds of motivation — learn the process; personal or institutional curiosity; academic activity

Team's Purpose

Certified by Uday on 2026-10-25 at 06:59 UTC (L000251); reason, as given: "broadened for feasibility"

Uday (summarized): focus — which kind of weight is lost: fat, muscle or body water, within all adults without diabetes, whatever the drug is given for, any country; Asian people as a subgroup; kinds of purpose — research gaps; finding the right question; the review — a systematic review on this topic; with the results — decide whether a full review is worth it; maybe publish

Need for a Review

Certified by Uday on 2026-10-25 at 07:26 UTC (L000292); reason, as given: "broadened for feasibility"

Uday (summarized): case — new review: no review examined so far answers the focus, which kind of weight is lost: fat, muscle or body water, within all adults without diabetes, whatever the drug is given for, any country; Asian people as a subgroup; grounds — previous reviews; knowledge gaps; clinical justification (used off label for weight in Asia; muscle or water loss matters for older and lean patients; it matters for any adult without diabetes on these drugs); impact analysis (tell clinicians whether the weight lost is fat; show where trials are missing); uncertainties — whether one answer fits every reason for treatment; the drug's own effect, when trials give it with another drug; whether body scans can measure each part of a small loss; not searched — PROSPERO, OSF Registries, Cochrane, Epistemonikos; OpenAlex reviews past the first 100; full texts

Review Objective

Certified by Uday on 2026-10-25 at 07:34 UTC (L000304); reason, as given: "broadened for feasibility"

Uday (summarized): objectives — understand a topic; identify new areas for research; wording — to understand which kind of weight is lost with SGLT2 inhibitors (fat, muscle or body water) within all adults without diabetes, whatever the drug is given for, any country (Asian people as a subgroup), and to identify new areas for research: where trials are missing

Feasibility

Certified by Uday on 2026-10-25 at 06:47 UTC (L000232); reason, as given: "broaden is right"

Uday (summarized): decision — broaden: all adults without diabetes, any country; Asian people as a subgroup; kinds of feasibility — literature availability; FMEA (very few trials)

Research Question

Certified by Uday on 2026-10-25 at 08:13 UTC (L000370); reason, as given: "this is the question"

Uday (summarized): question — which kind of weight is lost with SGLT2 inhibitors, compared with placebo, no treatment or usual care: fat, muscle or body water, in adults without diabetes (Asian people as a subgroup); framework — PICO; context — any country, whatever the drug is given for; Asian people as a subgroup; population — adults without diabetes; people with prediabetes count as without diabetes; intervention — any SGLT2 inhibitor, no minimum treatment time; comparison — placebo, no treatment or usual care; outcomes — fat, muscle and body water; muscle as lean or fat-free mass, muscle itself, and also strength or physical performance; fat, lean mass, muscle itself, body water and strength each kept apart; the measuring method recorded for each study; inclusion and exclusion criteria — randomized controlled trials and controlled cohort studies; another treatment only when both groups get the same one; studies that also enrol people with diabetes only if they report those without diabetes apart; a pair of groups within a larger trial counts if it meets these criteria

Paper as an Object, Literature as a Tensor and Registering Evidence Dynamically: building them is not available in this prompt version (it is being developed for a dedicated planning tool), so they may not behave as intended here; any designs drafted at Step 7 are kept in the planning folder but were not applied to studies.

Search Strategy

Certified by Uday on 2026-10-25 at 10:08 UTC (L000489); reason, as given: "finds both known studies"

PubMed — the search shown in protocol item 10, records created up to 2026-10-25: SGLT2 inhibitors, by subject heading and each drug's name, AND fat, lean or muscle, body water, strength or performance, their measuring methods or body weight, AND randomized trials or cohort studies, animal-only records left out; ClinicalTrials.gov — the search shown in protocol item 10, any status: the drug names among treatments AND the parts of the weight among outcomes; OpenAlex — the planned search shown in protocol item 10, in titles and abstracts only, with the four body-weight phrases added, published up to the run date, run in the next session; WHO trial portal — the drug names of the ClinicalTrials.gov search, searched by Uday by hand in the next session; not searched — Embase and CINAHL, which Uday cannot search, named in the protocol with the reason, and other paid databases, out of reach; filters — no language, start-date or population limit, with adults, the comparison, diabetes status and Asian people judged at screening; known studies — PubMed 29235586, 33879970, 39361103 and 42104798, checked in PubMed and OpenAlex; stopping rule — the strings stop changing when every known study is found and none of the records added by the last change meets the question, as Uday screens them; peer review — none, as there is no second searcher; the protocol says the strings were not peer reviewed

Methodology

Certified by Uday on 2026-10-25 at 11:51 UTC (L000655); reason, as given: "fits a solo review"

Uday (summarized): type — a scoping review of every study that meets the question, then a systematic review of each part of the weight that reaches 3 or more similar randomized trials; sources — PubMed, ClinicalTrials.gov, OpenAlex and the WHO trial portal, as in the approved search, with Embase and CINAHL named as not searched; selecting studies — Uday alone screens titles and abstracts and reads full texts, with the assistant's suggested decision beside each record, and about 2 weeks later screens again a random tenth of all his decisions, drawn by code and without seeing his first decision, reporting how often the two passes agree; collecting data — Uday alone fills the approved extraction form, with the assistant's suggested values and their passages beside each; judging each study, systematic part only — risk of bias for each outcome, trials with RoB 2 and cohort studies with ROBINS-I, cohort studies judged and described there; pooling — a meta-analysis for a part only if 3 or more similar randomized trials report it, cohort studies not counted, otherwise results tabled and described part by part and reported with SWiM; how 'similar' is judged and how to pool settled if a part reaches 3; trust — GRADE for each part; Asian people — results described apart; reporting — PRISMA-ScR for the scoping part and PRISMA 2020 for the systematic part; limitations — one person screens, reads and judges

Certified again together with the protocol (section 2) on 2026-10-25 at 15:17 UTC (L000780); reason, as given: "follows the standard template"

2. Protocol

Which kind of weight is lost with SGLT2 inhibitors in adults without diabetes: fat, muscle or body water? A scoping review, with a systematic review of the parts with enough trials: protocol

Status: certified by Uday on 2026-10-25 with the reason “follows the standard template” (L000780). The protocol writes every decision Uday certified in full sentences, item by item in the order of the PRISMA-P 2015 checklist [34], with the searches reported as PRISMA-S asks [14], a numbered reference for each methodological choice, and three closing sections: what planning found, how STIR-L4 and AI were used, and gaps. '(my suggestion)' marks wording the assistant drafted that came from neither Uday nor a retrieved fact; Uday accepted it when he certified the protocol. The word GAP marks what is missing and who can supply it; every gap is listed again at the end. Planning-record ids (L followed by six digits) are given for audit; numbered references are listed at the end.

Administrative information

Item 1a. Identification

This document is the protocol of a scoping review, with a systematic review of each part of the weight that has enough trials. Title: Which kind of weight is lost with SGLT2 inhibitors in adults without diabetes: fat, muscle or body water? A scoping review, with a systematic review of the parts with enough trials: protocol (my suggestion, from Uday's focus and methods).

Item 1b. Update

This is a new review, not an update of an earlier review: Uday certified the need for a new review, because no review examined during planning answers the focus (record: L000292).

Item 2. Registration

The protocol is to be registered with OSF Registries (Uday's choice; record: L000679). GAP: registration number and date. None yet: on 2026-10-25 Uday chose not to submit the protocol, as planning was a test (record: L000708). If he submits it, he adds the number and date here. Facts shown when Uday chose the register: most of the scoping review protocols examined in a recent study of high-impact journals were registered on OSF [1]; PROSPERO is restricted to systematic reviews of the effects of interventions with a health-related outcome [2]; and much of what PRISMA-P recommends was not stated in PROSPERO registrations [3], so this full protocol is kept beside the register entry (my suggestion).

Item 3a. Authors and contact

Author: Uday, the name as he gave it, the only author. Institutional affiliation: left blank at Uday's request (record: L000679). GAP: e-mail address and postal address of the corresponding author. Uday adds them on the register's form when he submits; none is kept in the planning files.

Item 3b. Contributions

Uday planned the review and will screen the records, extract the data, judge each study and write the report; he is the guarantor of the review. The AI assistant is not an author (my suggestion).

Item 4. Amendments

This protocol is not an amendment; it has not been registered. After registration, a change is made only if Uday agrees to reopen the decision concerned; the changed decision is certified again with its reason, dated in the planning record and submitted to OSF Registries as an amendment.

Item 5a–5c. Support

5a. The review has no funding (Uday); no other support is named. 5b. There is no funder or sponsor (my suggestion, as there is no funding). 5c. No funder, sponsor or institution had a role in developing the protocol (my suggestion).

Introduction

Item 6. Rationale

Uday's topic is the role of SGLT2 inhibitors in the weight lost by all adults without diabetes, whatever the drug is given for, in any country, with Asian people as a subgroup; the agreed focus is which kind of weight is lost: fat, muscle or body water (records: L000250, L000243). Why it matters (Uday's clinical justification, summarized): these drugs are used off label for weight in Asia; losing muscle or water matters for older and lean patients; and it matters for any adult without diabetes who takes these drugs. What the review would change (Uday's expected impact, summarized): it would tell clinicians whether the weight lost is fat, and show where trials are missing (record: L000292). What is already known (FACT): of the 9 reviews found during planning that may bear on the focus, 7 name body fat, 0 name muscle or lean mass and 1 names body water, in their titles and abstracts. 3 of them are reviews of weight in people without diabetes or with and without it [4, 5, 6]; all 3 name body fat and none names muscle, lean mass or body water. Their search end, as their abstracts give it: May 2021 for PMID 34897070; not stated in the abstract (abstract only: unknown) for PMID 34542222; not stated in the abstract (abstract only: unknown) for PMID 42339604. The latest search end stated among the 9 is August 2025, in a review that the rule placed with the focus because it names body composition and without diabetes [7]. The nearest evidence, in Uday's words, is from reviews in people with type 2 diabetes (FACT, from their abstracts; no full text was read). By a declared rule over the review records examined during planning (type 2 diabetes named as the population, muscle or lean mass named, and a systematic review or meta-analysis by its title; record: L000771), 6 reviews were found; none names adults without diabetes. In a meta-analysis of 8 randomized trials with 541 participants (its title names older adults) [8] (2026), fat mass fell (standardized mean difference −0.53); muscle mass fell less (standardized mean difference −0.35), which the authors call substantially smaller than the fat loss. In a network meta-analysis of 33 randomized trials with 5,399 participants [9] (2026), fat mass fell consistently (for example dapagliflozin −1.75 kg); lean mass fell modestly with empagliflozin (−1.22 kg), ipragliflozin (−0.99 kg) and canagliflozin (−0.80 kg) but not with dapagliflozin or tofogliflozin, and no SGLT2 inhibitor significantly reduced skeletal muscle mass compared with placebo; strength and physical performance were rarely reported. In a meta-analysis of 5 randomized trials of at least 12 months with 695 participants [10] (2026), fat mass fell (−1.26 kg); fat-free mass did not differ significantly (−0.53 kg; 95% confidence interval −1.62 to 0.56). In a meta-analysis of 19 randomized trials with 1,482 participants [11] (2023), fat is not reported in the abstract; lean body mass fell by 0.66 and skeletal muscle mass by 0.35 (units not given); the quality of the evidence was low. In a meta-analysis of 25 randomized trials with 2,286 participants [12] (2023), fat mass fell (−1.49); lean mass (−0.80), skeletal muscle mass (−0.38) and skeletal muscle index (−0.12) fell as well; body water fell (−0.96); units are not given in the abstract. In a meta-analysis of 18 studies with 1,430 participants [13] (2022), fat mass fell (−1.16 kg); lean mass changed by −0.76 kg (95% confidence interval −1.53 to 0.01) and skeletal muscle mass fell (−1.01 kg). Their search end, as their abstracts give it: not stated in the abstract (abstract only: unknown) [8]; not stated in the abstract (abstract only: unknown) [9]; April 22, 2024 [10]; 1 May 2023 [11]; February 2023 [12]; not stated in the abstract (abstract only: unknown) [13]. The abstract of [10] names type 2 diabetes only as the condition in which these drugs are used; the people in its trials are not described there. Reviews were searched in PubMed and OpenAlex only (see What planning found). Uncertainties Uday recorded with the need (summarized): whether one answer fits every reason for treatment; the drug's own effect, when trials give it with another drug; and whether body scans can measure each part of a small loss.

Item 7. Objectives

The objectives (certified; Uday, summarized) are to understand which kind of weight is lost with SGLT2 inhibitors (fat, muscle or body water) within all adults without diabetes, whatever the drug is given for, in any country (Asian people as a subgroup), and to identify new areas for research: where trials are missing (record: L000304). The question (certified; framework PICO) is: which kind of weight is lost with SGLT2 inhibitors, compared with placebo, no treatment or usual care: fat, muscle or body water, in adults without diabetes (Asian people as a subgroup) (record: L000370). Participants: adults without diabetes; people with prediabetes count as without diabetes. Intervention: any SGLT2 inhibitor, with no minimum treatment time. Comparators: placebo, no treatment or usual care. Outcomes: fat, muscle and body water; muscle as lean or fat-free mass and muscle itself, and also strength or physical performance; each kept apart.

Methods

Item 8. Eligibility criteria

Study characteristics (certified; record: L000370). Participants: adults without diabetes, whatever the drug is given for and in any country; people with prediabetes count as without diabetes; a study that also enrols people with diabetes is eligible only if it reports those without diabetes apart. Intervention and comparators: any SGLT2 inhibitor, with no minimum treatment time, compared with placebo, no treatment or usual care; another treatment is allowed only when both groups get the same one, and a pair of groups within a larger trial counts if it meets these criteria. Outcomes: whole-body fat, lean or fat-free mass, muscle itself, body water, and strength or physical performance, each kept apart, with the measuring method recorded for each study. Designs: randomized controlled trials and controlled cohort studies. Report characteristics: no language limit and no start-date limit (certified search strategy; record: L000489); records up to the search date; any publication status, including results posted in trial registers (my suggestion).

Item 9. Information sources

The review searches the following sources (certified search strategy; record: L000489). PubMed, through the NCBI E-utilities, records created from 1800/01/01 to the search date; searched on 2026-10-25. ClinicalTrials.gov, through its API version 2.0.5, studies of any status; searched on 2026-10-25, with data as of 2026-10-23T09:00:04 as the register stated. OpenAlex, through its works endpoint, in titles and abstracts, works published up to the run date: planned, not yet run; it was refused on 2026-10-25 because the free daily allowance of the network was used up and no access code is set (records: L000457, L000593). The WHO International Clinical Trials Registry Platform (the WHO trial portal), searched by Uday by hand with the drug names of the ClinicalTrials.gov search: planned, not yet run. Not searched: Embase and CINAHL, which Uday cannot search (no access), and other paid databases, which are out of reach. The review will not contact study authors or investigators and will not check reference lists (my suggestion). Dates of coverage: from the earliest record in each source to the search date. GAP: run dates and counts for OpenAlex and the WHO trial portal. Not run when planning ended; Uday supplies them when he runs these searches (OpenAlex reads past its first 100 results only with a free access code).

Item 10. Search strategy

The searches are reported as PRISMA-S asks [14]: each source and platform, the date searched, the full string, limits and filters, the known-study check and peer review.

Blocks: 1. SGLT2 inhibitors, by subject heading and each drug's name; 2. fat, lean or muscle, body water, strength or performance, their measuring methods or body weight; 3. randomized trials or cohort studies; records about animals only are left out (certified).

Search development (FACT): the first PubMed search returned 739 records (record: L000424) and missed a 2026 trial that Uday keeps as a known study [15]; adding four phrases for body weight gave the search above, which returned 1,287 more records and lost none of the first search's records (record: L000461). Known-study check (FACT): the PubMed search retrieves 4 of the 4 studies Uday confirmed as known studies [16, 17, 15, 18] (record: L000460); the same check is planned for OpenAlex. Peer review: none; Uday has no second searcher, so the strings were not peer reviewed (no PRESS check). Stopping rule (certified): the strings stop changing when every known study is found and none of the records added by the last change meets the question, as Uday screens them. Records and duplicates: every page of every response is saved unchanged with its sha256 check code; duplicates were removed by code (PubMed identifier, then DOI, then normalized title and year, with title-and-year matches confirmed by Uday): 2,566 records before and 2,565 after (record: L000517).

Item 11a. Data management

Every search result, screening decision, extracted value and certification is kept in the planning folder: saved results with their sha256 check codes, a record of every step that is never edited, and dated versions under version control; licensed full texts are kept out of any shared copy (certified; record: L000703).

Item 11b. Selection process

Uday alone screens titles and abstracts and reads full texts, with the assistant's suggested decision and its supporting passage beside each record; only Uday admits or excludes a record. About two weeks later he screens again a random tenth of all his decisions, drawn by code and without seeing his first decision, and reports how often the two passes agree (certified; record: L000655). Uday's reason, as he gave it when he certified the methods: 'fits a solo review'; he plans the review alone, with about 5 hours a week (team details, summarized). Published evidence bearing on this choice: single-reviewer screening of titles and abstracts misses more relevant studies than dual screening [19, 20]; false exclusions under single-reviewer screening fall most often on nonrandomized studies of interventions [21]; one reviewer's full-text screening is less sensitive than two reviewers' [22]; and evidence comparing AI tools as second screeners is limited [23].

Item 11c. Data collection process

Uday alone fills the certified data-extraction form, with the assistant's suggested values and the exact passage for each beside it (certified; record: L000655). Data will not be sought or confirmed from investigators (my suggestion). GAP: whether the extraction form is piloted before use, and on how many studies. Not decided; Uday decides.

Item 12. Data items

For each study the certified data-extraction form (record: L000380), records: PubMed and DOI identifiers; one study key linking every report of the same study; design: randomized controlled trial (parallel or crossover) or controlled cohort; how groups were formed; who was blinded; trial registration number; funding source; country where the study was done (also one of the two marks of the Asian subgroup, kept apart from race or ethnicity); number enrolled and analysed in each group; age; percent women; diabetes status: share with prediabetes; if people with diabetes were also enrolled, whether results for those without diabetes are given apart; Asian subgroup: race or ethnicity as the study reports it, kept apart from the country where the study was done; any results given for Asian participants apart; what the drug was given for (the condition); starting weight and body mass index; which SGLT2 inhibitor, dose and treatment time; other treatment both groups get (for example usual medicines or a diet); placebo, no treatment or usual care, as the study describes it; total weight change; whole-body fat: fat mass, percent body fat; fat in particular places: not recorded - whole-body fat only (fat under the skin, around the belly organs or inside organs such as the liver or heart is not recorded); lean or fat-free mass; muscle itself: muscle mass, area or volume; body water: total; outside or inside cells and plasma volume when reported; strength; physical performance, each as the study tests it; the share of the weight lost that is fat, lean or water, when the study gives it; measuring method; every time point; for each group the number analysed, the starting value and the change or end value with its spread; the difference between groups with its confidence interval; how missing data were handled. A value that a study does not report is recorded as not reported, never guessed; each value keeps its exact passage, where it is in the paper, and whether it is reported, not reported or unclear.

Item 13. Outcomes and prioritization

Main outcomes (from the certified question): whole-body fat (fat mass, percent body fat), lean or fat-free mass, muscle itself (mass, area or volume) and body water, each kept apart. Additional outcomes: strength or physical performance, and total weight change (my suggestion: the question names strength with 'also', and the form records total weight). Rationale: the focus asks which kind of weight is lost. For every outcome the measuring method and every time point are recorded.

Item 14. Risk of bias in individual studies

For the systematic part only, risk of bias is judged for each outcome: randomized trials with RoB 2 [24] and cohort studies with ROBINS-I [25]; cohort studies are judged and described there (certified; record: L000655). The judgments feed the GRADE rating of each part of the weight (item 17). Uday makes these judgments alone; agreement between raters using RoB 2 was low in one study [26].

Item 15a. Criteria for quantitative synthesis

A part of the weight is pooled in a meta-analysis only if 3 or more similar randomized trials report it; cohort studies are not counted toward the 3 (certified; record: L000655). Published guidance bearing on this rule: heterogeneity cannot be reliably estimated when very few studies are available [27]; with very few studies, refraining from a quantitative meta-analysis may be the best solution [28]; and whether to pool cohort studies with randomized trials affects the certainty of evidence and needs guidance [29].

Item 15b. Summary measures and methods of combining

GAP: summary measures, handling of data, the method of combining studies, the measure of consistency, and how 'similar' trials are judged. Not decided; if a part reaches 3 similar randomized trials, Uday settles them and registers them as an amendment before any pooling (certified rule; registering them as an amendment was my suggestion).

Item 15c. Additional analyses

Results for Asian participants are described apart (certified). No other subgroup or sensitivity analyses are planned (my suggestion).

Item 15d. If quantitative synthesis is not appropriate

The scoping part covers every study that meets the question. Where a part of the weight is not pooled, results are tabled and described part by part and reported with SWiM [30] (certified; record: L000655).

Item 16. Meta-bias(es)

For each trial, the outcomes in its register entry are compared with those it reports (my suggestion). No statistical test for publication bias is planned, as few trials are expected (my suggestion).

Item 17. Confidence in cumulative evidence

The certainty of the evidence for each part of the weight is rated with GRADE (certified), following the Core GRADE guidance on risk of bias and publication bias [31].

Reporting and limitations (after item 17)

The scoping part will be reported with PRISMA-ScR [32] and the systematic part with PRISMA 2020 [33] (certified). This protocol follows PRISMA-P 2015 [34], whose authors encourage its use, as applicable, for any type of review protocol, and its searches follow PRISMA-S [14]. Limitations (certified): one person screens, reads and judges; the assistant's suggestions are not checked by a second person; and the author of the prompt and kit uses them and judges their suggestions alone.

What planning found

Every statement in this section is a FACT read by code from the planning record and the saved search results.

Existing reviews (need for a review). Reviews were searched in PubMed (with the systematic-review filter) and OpenAlex (works typed as reviews); each search is listed below with its count (records, not studies). After duplicates were removed by code, 241 review records remained (record: L000259); by a declared rule over titles and abstracts, 9 may bear on the focus, 37 cover the population with other outcomes, 44 cover the focus in other populations, 38 name fat or muscle only in another sense (for example liver fat), and 113 neither. What the 9 that may bear on the focus cover is summarized under item 6, with what 6 of those on the focus in other populations, reviews in people with type 2 diabetes, found on fat and on lean mass or muscle. Not searched for reviews: PROSPERO, OSF Registries, Cochrane and Epistemonikos; OpenAlex results past the first 100 of each search were not read, and no full text was read.

Soundness of the question. Of the 13 PubMed records tagged as randomized controlled trials in people without diabetes (any region; record: L000262), the reasons for treatment named in their titles are: overweight or obesity 4; fatty liver 3; none of these 1; prediabetes or insulin resistance 2; kidney disease 1; heart failure 2; polycystic ovary syndrome 1. None names fat, muscle and body water together (record: L000279).

Feasibility. PubMed counts on 2026-10-25 (records): the objective as then certified (fat, muscle or body water in Asian people without diabetes): 5; the same in any region: 154; trial records only, of the latter: 51. ClinicalTrials.gov on 2026-10-25: 473 studies naming the drug terms among interventions, with a status of recruiting, not yet recruiting, enrolling by invitation or active, not recruiting (record: L000198); of these, 36 name an SGLT2 inhibitor among interventions and fat, muscle or body water among outcomes, and 23 of those do not name diabetes among their conditions (columns of rule step5-rule-2; record: L000205). Time (computed from Uday's answers; record: L000215): 92 days, 13.1 weeks, about 66 hours at 5 hours a week. Uday certified broadening to all adults without diabetes in any country, with Asian people as a subgroup (record: L000232).

Searches for the review. PubMed: 2,026 records; ClinicalTrials.gov: 540 registered studies (completed 225; recruiting 103; unknown 83; not yet recruiting 51; active not recruiting 40; terminated 22; withdrawn 13; enrolling by invitation 3; with results posted: 90). Known studies: 4 of 4 retrieved by the PubMed search. Records to screen: 2,566 before duplicates and 2,565 after. OpenAlex and the WHO trial portal: not run.

Pilot screening (a test of the criteria). To test the eligibility criteria, Uday screened 26 records by title and abstract: 4 met the criteria, 12 were unclear and 10 did not (record: L000772).

How STIR-L4 and AI were used

This section follows the reporting items of the 2025 position statement on AI use in evidence synthesis [35], which supports the RAISE recommendations [36].

Framework, prompt and kit. Planning used STIR-L4 [37], which represents planning as ten elements joined by 27 directed flows, with each decision certified by a named person. The assistant followed the STIR-L4 planning prompt and its helper kit, in development versions made before release 1.0; each file is saved unchanged with its sha256 check code (records: L000003, L000107, L000735, L000006, L000736).

AI model. Anthropic Claude Opus 5.5 (model id claude-opus-5-5), as Uday read it from the run settings, in sessions 1 to 5 (records: L000738, L000739, L000740, L000741, L000742, L000743). Settings such as temperature: not available. Dates: 2026-10-25 (session 1 03:01 to 03:58 UTC; session 2 05:01 to 07:45 UTC; session 3 07:47 to 11:00 UTC; session 4 11:05 to 12:51 UTC; session 5 from 14:28 UTC).

What the AI did, by step. Topic, interests, focus and purpose: recorded Uday's answers and reflected them back in his words, and drafted the kinds of motivation and purpose for him to choose. Need for a review: ran and logged searches for existing reviews in PubMed and OpenAlex, grouped the records by a declared rule, read search end dates from abstracts, appraised the soundness of the question from logged records, and drafted the case. Objective: drafted its wording from Uday's answers. Feasibility: counted records for candidate searches, listed ongoing studies in ClinicalTrials.gov and computed the time available. Question: looked up Medical Subject Headings and counted what trial records name, and drafted the question. Before the search: drafted the extraction form, the features to count and the evidence map. Search strategy: ran the PubMed and ClinicalTrials.gov searches, checked the known studies and drafted the stopping rule. Pilot screening: removed duplicates by code, ordered the records and suggested a decision with its supporting passage for each record Uday screened. Methods and protocol: retrieved published guidance, estimated the hours the methods need, and drafted the methods and this protocol. Across planning the record holds 169 logged retrievals, 192 computations and 89 drafts.

What people decided. Uday certified each of the 11 decisions (16 certification records, some after a reopening, each with a reason in his own words): the motivations, the team's purpose, the need for a review, the objective, feasibility, the question, the extraction form, the features to count, the evidence map, the search strategy and the methods with the protocol. He confirmed the known studies and the duplicate match, made all 26 screening decisions of the pilot screening and chose not to submit the protocol (a test). The assistant admitted or excluded no record and certified nothing.

How the AI's work was checked. Every count in this protocol is read from the planning record, where it was computed by code from the saved response; the saved results were re-counted by code against the record at the end of sessions, with the results kept in the planning record. Every passage quoted to Uday was checked by code against the saved title, abstract or full text. Uday read each draft before he certified it. No second person checked the assistant's suggestions.

Use during the review (certified): the assistant will run and log searches, count records by code, suggest screening decisions and extracted values with their exact passages, and draft wording; Uday makes and certifies every decision, with his reason recorded.

Interests and sharing (certified): Uday wrote the STIR-L4 planning prompt and its helper kit used here (summarized); no other interest is declared (my suggestion). The prompt, kit, record of every step and code are shared with the review (my suggestion); a 2026 study of reviews that disclose AI use found that sharing of prompts and human-validation procedures was insufficient [38].

Limitations. The assistant's suggestions are not checked by a second person; the author of the prompt and kit uses them and judges their suggestions alone; evidence on AI tools as second screeners is limited [23].

Gaps

  • - Item 2: registration number and date. None yet: on 2026-10-25 Uday chose not to submit the protocol, as planning was a test (record: L000708). If he submits it, he adds the number and date here.
  • - Item 3a: e-mail address and postal address of the corresponding author. Uday adds them on the register's form when he submits; none is kept in the planning files.
  • - Item 9: run dates and counts for OpenAlex and the WHO trial portal. Not run when planning ended; Uday supplies them when he runs these searches (OpenAlex reads past its first 100 results only with a free access code).
  • - Item 10: the exact string for the WHO trial portal, built from the drug names of the ClinicalTrials.gov search when Uday runs it by hand.
  • - Item 11c: whether the extraction form is piloted before use, and on how many studies. Not decided; Uday decides.
  • - Item 15b: summary measures, handling of data, the method of combining studies, the measure of consistency, and how 'similar' trials are judged. Not decided; if a part reaches 3 similar randomized trials, Uday settles them and registers them as an amendment before any pooling (certified rule; registering them as an amendment was my suggestion).

DRAFT: is your question sound? (yours to challenge)What the records showStill uncertain
Clinically: do Asian people without diabetes take these drugs, and does the outcome matter?Without diabetes, the records name heart failure (#1) and kidney disease, in Singapore (#2), as uses. Off-label use is stated for obesity by a review (#3), and for a person without diabetes in India by a case report (#4). Muscle loss is a stated worry: an earlier review, of people with diabetes, urges careful monitoring in older adults [17].How often clinicians in Asia prescribe these drugs for weight; #3 and #4 state such use but do not measure it.
Scientifically: is there a mechanism and earlier evidence, and can the outcome be measured?Glucose lost in urine creates an energy deficit, but eating more limits the weight lost; there is also a short-lived loss of salt and water in urine (#5). The body's responses are similar without diabetes but smaller (#6). Two earlier reviews of people without diabetes found modest weight loss (1.62 kg more than placebo in one) and no significant change in fat mass [17]. Heavy-water dilution and magnetic resonance (#8; there TBW is total body water, D2O heavy water and RE one of the drugs), impedance, a weak electric current (#9), and DXA, a low-dose X-ray scan (#10), are used; lean-mass estimates can shift with body water and do not measure muscle directly, so strength or performance is also needed (#10).Whether the small loss without diabetes splits as it does with diabetes, where fat was over 70% and extracellular water about 20% (#9). Studies of these drugs and muscle loss were mostly in people with diabetes (68%) and Japanese people (53%) (#1). I read abstracts only.
Logically: do population, drug, outcome and purpose fit?1) "Fat or muscle" leaves out water, which is part of the weight lost (#8, #9). 2) Within your population, search 3 found 4 records [8]: 3 reviews and 1 case report [18]; without the Asian limit, search 2 found 135 [7]. A trial in people without diabetes with a Japanese affiliation (#7) names no country in its abstract and was found only by search 5 [18], so these words miss some studies. 3) No comparison is named yet; it comes with the research question. A thin evidence base fits your purpose (research gaps) and the impact you expect.How many trials exist; Feasibility will count them.
PlainlyYour question is clinically and scientifically reasonable and measurable, but it assumes two things the records question: that weight is lost only as fat or muscle (part is water, and lean mass is not muscle), and that these drugs are used for weight in your population (the uses these records name without diabetes are heart failure and kidney disease; off-label use is stated, not measured).You decide whether and how to reframe.

Rule (my terms, yours to edit)a record may bear if it names fat, muscle or body water in their own sense and people without diabetes; Asian words mark the subgroup
DRAFT: does your wider question hold up? (yours to challenge)What the records showStill uncertain
ClinicallyWithout diabetes, these drugs are recommended in heart failure (shown earlier). In a review of people without diabetes, those with heart failure had a 20% lower risk of cardiovascular death or heart-failure admission than those not treated (PMID 33625242) [10]. In heart failure, losing water may be part of the benefit: “Fluid volume changes might be an important mechanism underlying the beneficial clinical effects” (PMID 33357505) [9]. Muscle loss is a stated worry, but the evidence is mixed: muscle mass fell in 15 studies and did not change in 6, and physical performance did not change (PMID 40707349) [9].How many people without diabetes take these drugs for weight; most muscle studies were in people with diabetes (shown earlier).
ScientificallyWater: in a heart-failure trial (88% without diabetes), estimated plasma volume fell 7.3% more than with placebo (PMID 33357505) [9]; in kidney disease without diabetes, dapagliflozin lowered blood pressure without raising salt and water output in urine, in a study of six patients (PMID 35478433) [9]. Fat: fat under the skin fell in heart failure without diabetes (PMID 34325888) [9]. The total to split is small: body weight 1.21 kg lower than without the drug, in the review above (PMID 33625242) [10].Whether body-composition scans can split a loss this small; I have not searched for how precise they are. I read abstracts only.
Logically1) Of the 13 records tagged as randomized trials (searched as people without diabetes, any region), the 4 in overweight or obesity all name fat and none names water; of the 3 in heart failure or kidney disease, 2 name water [8]. One pooled answer could mix water lost as part of treatment with water lost as a side effect. 2) 3 of the 4 obesity trials give the SGLT2 inhibitor together with another drug, exenatide or topiramate [8], [9]; in one, the loss was mostly fat with no lean change (PMID 27550386) [9], but that cannot be credited to the SGLT2 inhibitor alone. The comparison is settled with the research question. 3) None of the 13 names fat, muscle and water together [8], or an Asian population [11].Full texts may report parts that abstracts leave out.
PlainlyYour question is reasonable and can be measured. But it assumes that weight lost means the same thing whatever the drug is given for. The records suggest it does not: in heart failure, water loss may be part of the benefit; when weight is the aim, it is a side effect. And 3 of the 4 weight trials test the drug with another drug.You decide whether and how to reframe.

Type. A scoping review maps every study that meets your question: which parts of the weight were measured, how, in whom, and where trials are missing. A systematic review then judges and sums up the parts with enough studies (open: question 1).you (summarized)
Sources. As you approved: PubMed, ClinicalTrials.gov, OpenAlex (still to run) and the WHO trial portal (you, by hand); Embase and CINAHL named as not searched.your approved search
Selecting studies. You alone screen titles and abstracts and read full texts; you decide each record, with my suggested decision beside it (my suggestion: as now). Later you screen a random tenth of all your decisions again (257 so far [19]), drawn by code, without seeing your first decision, about 2 weeks later, and report how often the two passes agree (my suggestions; the 2-week gap as one 2026 review did, #1). My reading of the 88 abstracts: none measures what such a re-check catches.you; [16]
Collecting data. You alone fill the extraction form you approved, with my suggested values and their exact passages beside each (my suggestion). The form needs no change for these methods.your approved form
Judging each study (systematic part only). Risk of bias for each outcome: trials with RoB 2, the revised tool for randomized trials (#5); cohort studies with ROBINS-I, its counterpart for non-randomized studies (#6) (my suggestion).[16]
Pooling. For each part of the weight, a meta-analysis only if 3 or more similar randomized trials report it; otherwise results tabled and described part by part, reported with SWiM [20]. How "similar" is judged, and how to pool: settled if a part reaches 3 (my suggestion). Cohort studies do not count toward the 3; how they are used: question 2.you; [20]
FACT: counting randomized trials only (by title, abstract or record type), your 3 included trials name fat 1, body water 1, function 1; with your unsure ones, 12 trials: fat 4, body water 5, function 3, lean 1 [18].[18]
Trust in each result. Certainty for each part with GRADE, a standard way to rate how far evidence can be trusted, where trials start high and cohort studies low (#7) (my suggestion).[16]
Asian people. Results for Asian participants described apart.your approved question
Reporting. Scoping part with PRISMA-ScR, systematic part with PRISMA 2020: reporting checklists for each kind (#9, #10) (my suggestion).[16]
Limitations. One person screens, reads and judges. One reviewer's full-text screening missed relevant studies in a 2026 study (#2); raters agreed only slightly on overall risk of bias in 2020 (#3).you; [16]

13 OutcomesMain: whole-body fat, lean or fat-free mass, muscle itself, body water; additional: strength or performance, total weight
14 Risk of biasSystematic part only, per outcome: RoB 2 for trials and ROBINS-I for cohort studies (tools for judging how far a study's design may bias its result)
15a When to poolA meta-analysis only for a part with 3 or more similar randomized trials, with published guidance on very few studies
15b How to poolGAP: summary measures, handling of data, the method of combining studies, the measure of consistency, and how 'similar' trials are judged. Not decided; if a part reaches 3 similar randomized trials, Uday settles them and registers them as an amendment before any pooling (certified rule; registering them as an amendment was my suggestion).
15c Other analysesAsian participants described apart; no other subgroup or sensitivity analyses
15d Without poolingResults tabled part by part and reported with SWiM (a guideline for reporting results without meta-analysis)
16 Meta-biasRegistered outcomes compared with reported ones; no statistical test for publication bias
17 ConfidenceGRADE (a system for rating how much to trust the evidence) for each part
Reporting, limitationsPRISMA-ScR and PRISMA 2020 (reporting guidelines for scoping and systematic reviews); one person screens, reads and judges
